# FORK-PLAN §7 progress log: archive

Older entries moved out of FORK-PLAN.md §7 to keep it small (§1a.55). Oldest first; the newest entries stay in FORK-PLAN.md.

- **2026-10-07/08, Phase 0.1 (done):** system recorded in `docs/fork-benchmarks.md` (vainfo,
  vulkaninfo, ffmpeg, CPU i5-11600K, 32 GB, NVMe). VCN3: HEVC Main/Main10 decode + encode up to
  8192×4352, 4:2:0 only; RADV exposes video decode/encode queues (families 2 and 3) and
  `VK_KHR_video_decode_h265` / `encode_h265`, plus `external_memory_dma_buf` and
  `image_drm_format_modifier`. Note: an Intel iGPU is also present (renderD128, Vulkan GPU1);
  the AMD card is renderD129 / Vulkan GPU0.
- **Phase 0.2 (done):** synthetic fixtures generated by `xm5.rs` specs (5 clips, ≈ 12 GB).
  `/home` was 93 % full: deleted `target/debug/incremental` (rebuildable cache, +14 GB).
- **Bench infrastructure:** new `xm5` section in `cargo xtask bench` (not in the default set):
  ceilings (ffmpeg), decode, play-full, play-graded, jkl, scrub, multicut, export 4K/4K
  graded/6K, 422-play, with GPU busy / VRAM / RSS sampling. Fixed for Linux: load average
  (`/proc/loadavg`), the bench's wgpu adapter is now HighPerformance (as eframe), `Bench.speed`
  for JKL, `build_sequence_at` for 29.97 sequences.
- **Pre-existing CI failures on Linux, fixed:** (1) `shortcuts_tests::assign_reassigns_conflicts_and_undoes`
  assumed the Mac keymap (Linux default keymap has 3 conflicts of its own: Ctrl+9,
  Ctrl+Shift+M, Ctrl+T; left as is, test now counts only the new conflict). (2)
  `fx_tests::gpu_effects_match_cpu_exactly` failed on RADV and Intel: at 1:1 the compositor's
  interpolated sample position is off by float rounding, so bilinear filtering mixed a trace of
  the neighbouring texel in; `load4` in `composite.wgsl` now snaps offsets < 1/512 px.
- **Phase 0.3 (done, synthetic):** baseline in `docs/fork-benchmarks.md` "Phase 0". Ceilings:
  6.2K decode 43.2 fps on GPU / 34.0 with download; HEVC Main10 encode 29.9 fps at 4K, 19.5 at
  6.2K (lower bounds: decode in the same process). App: media-stack decode 16.2 fps at 84 ms
  CPU/frame; **play-full, graded, multicut and JKL show 0 frames** (every job ~550 ms late and
  cancelled); scrub p50 1.8 s / p95 2.7 s; export 4K 5.1 fps, graded 0.7 fps (23 GB RSS
  peak), 6K 3.9 fps; 4:2:2 does not decode at all (placeholder frames). Multicut peaked at
  11.3 GB VRAM of 12.
- **Phase 0.4 (done):** the earlier "59 fps" was `decode_bench` on the 4K60 10-bit fixture
  (decoder only, ffmpeg pipe + download + CPU deinterleave): 59.8 fps vs 162.5 fps ffmpeg with
  frames on the GPU (37 %) and 69.8 fps for FilmCraft's own software decoder on this CPU.
- **Findings for later phases:** (a) the GOP cache is not ~13 frames at 6.2K as §3.2 assumes:
  a cache in use keeps `MIN_FRAMES` = 64 frames whatever their size (≈ 5 GB at 6.2K), only
  idle caches fall under `FRAME_BUDGET`; Phase 1.4 must bound that too. (b) The playback
  scheduler shows nothing (instead of fewer frames) when decode is slower than real time; Phase 3.
  (c) 4:2:2 has no decoder at all on Linux today; Phase 6.1 (ffmpeg software path) is a hard
  requirement, not an optimisation. (d) `mixer_tests::perf_24_tracks_3_effects_realtime_factor`
  needs ≥ 4× real time on one core: 4.7× idle, 3.0× under load; run CI on an idle machine.

- **Phase 1.1 (done):** `PixelData::Yuv16Bi { y, uv, chroma, bits }` (P010 / P210, MSB-aligned);
  CPU paths go through `VideoFrame::to_planar()`; the VA-API reader fills it straight from the
  pipe (no deinterleave thread for 10-bit). Parity: VA-API still bit-exact with the software
  decoder (`tests/vaapi.rs`, compared through `to_planar`).
- **Phase 1.2 (done):** 9–16-bit planes upload as `R16Unorm` / `Rg16Unorm` when the device has
  `TEXTURE_FORMAT_16BIT_NORM` (requested by the app via `filmcraft_ui_egui::wgpu_configuration()`,
  the bench and the tests); half floats remain the fallback. New `texel_scale` uniform (p3.z) and
  source kind 3 (biplanar) in `composite.wgsl`. **Upstream bug found and fixed:** 10/12-bit
  limited-range offsets were scaled by 2^bits/255 instead of 2^(bits-8) (black 64.25, range 879.4
  for 10-bit: every 10-bit clip ≈ 0.4 % dim in code values, 1–2 % in linear light on the GPU path);
  8-bit full range had the same off-by-one. GPU now matches the CPU within 1e-3 linear on AMD and
  Intel, both upload paths (`tests::biplanar_and_16bit_uploads_match_the_cpu`).
- **Phase 1.3 (done):** planes read whole into pooled buffers; `pool::take_u16_filled` skips
  re-zeroing a recycled 78 MB plane. Pipe size experiment: 64 KiB vs 1 MiB pipe made no difference
  (22.2 vs 21.8 fps): the cap is ffmpeg's serial download + write, not the pipe.
- **Phase 1.4 (done):** decode cache from installed RAM (`gop::auto_limits`: RAM/5 in 1–16 GiB →
  6.4 GB here; live decoders RAM/8 GiB in 2–8 → 3: 31.1 GiB reported), per-cache floor 24–64 frames by frame size
  (was 64 always: ≈ 5 GB per 6.2K clip in use), Settings ▸ Memory ▸ "Decoded source frames
  (0 = Auto)" (`memory.decodeCacheMb`), `perf.stats` `cacheBudgetMB` + `maxLiveDecoders`.
- **Phase 1 acceptance (synthetic, quick pass):** CPU ms/frame −59 % in play-full (45.8 → 18.9),
  −66 % in decode (84 → 26–29) ✓ (≥ 40 %); parity tests ✓; "play-full drops nothing" ✗ on this
  path: decode 18–20 fps vs the 22 fps pipe ceiling (§1a.5, accepted on that basis; real time is

- **Phase 2.2b + 2.3 + 2.4 (done, synthetic quick pass):** zero-copy decode → display.
  `PixelData::Gpu(Arc<dyn GpuSurface>)` (frame crate: format, coded size, crop origin, DMA-BUF
  export, fallible `download`, `liveness` token; CPU paths read back through `to_planar`).
  `va.rs`: surfaces exported once as DRM PRIME (separate layers), each surface's memory has a
  process-unique id + liveness (libva reuses surface ids), pools keep ≤ 4 spare surfaces and
  destroy the rest. `crates/gpu/src/interop.rs` (only `unsafe` module of the gpu crate; crate lints
  now `deny` + `undocumented_unsafe_blocks`, per ADR 0002): `create_device` (wgpu-hal
  `open_with_callback` + `VK_KHR_external_memory_fd`, `VK_EXT_external_memory_dma_buf`,
  `VK_EXT_image_drm_format_modifier`), `Importer` (plane → `VkImage` with DRM modifier → wgpu
  texture, cached, dropped when the surface dies). The app (`wgpu_configuration` →
  `WgpuSetup::Existing`, opt-out `FILMCRAFT_NO_GPU_IMPORT`) and the bench use that device.
  `tests/va_interop.rs` (ui-egui): drawn-in-place == read-back on every frame (Main and Main10).
  Registered: in-process VA-API HEVC first, ffmpeg pipe as the next hardware decoder
  (`HybridDecoder::with_fallback` chain), software last; `perf.stats` `decode.hardware.backends`
  + `lastFallback`. VRAM accounting: GOP caches charge GPU frames their memory (`gpu_bytes`), a
  VRAM budget (Auto = VRAM/4 = 3.2 GB here, Settings ▸ Memory ▸ "Decoded frames in GPU memory",
  env `FILMCRAFT_VRAM_CACHE_MB`), and the monitor frame cache charges plans for GPU frames too
  (it held unlimited surfaces before: 9.4 GB). Results (quick pass, synthetic): **decode 41.3 fps =
  96 % of the 43.2 fps ceiling, 1.5 ms CPU/frame**; **play-full 240/0 shown/dropped, 2.1 ms
  CPU/frame, 4.75 GB VRAM total, RSS 0.2 GB**; scrub p50 0.5 s / p95 1.5 s; multicut 346/13 (drops
  at cuts; Phase 2 acceptance needs 0) at 11.0 GB VRAM peak; JKL L 2× and reverse still drop
  (Phase 3). Decisions: per-clip floor stays 24 frames (16 doubled multicut drops: measured);
  VRAM/4 not VRAM/2 (VRAM/2 exhausted the card with four 6.2K clips); the monitor frame cache
  charges a GPU frame 16 MB (full 78 MB left room for ~9 plans, fewer than the prefetch window);
  **dead end:** requesting the first frame after the next cut early (20–60 frames past the
  prefetch window) made multicut much worse (274/86 – 145/215 vs 346/13 without), removed.
- **Phase 2 checks (synthetic):** real app smoke test (control channel, 6.2K clip on a sequence):
  in-process VA-API, 223/0 shown/dropped, UI 60 fps, Program monitor shows the picture. Fallback
  chain test (`tests/vaapi.rs::in_process_failure_continues_on_the_pipe_then_in_software`):
  in-process failing mid-stream → ffmpeg pipe → software, output identical to software decode;
  **bug found and fixed:** after a hand-over to a second hardware decoder, pictures the first had
  returned came out again (hardware output now goes through `merge`). Pulled hardware:
  `FILMCRAFT_VAAPI_DEVICE=/dev/null` → software decode 13.7 fps, no crash; `FILMCRAFT_NO_GPU_IMPORT`
  → read-back path 240/0. Scrub (50 seeks): **p50 410 ms, p95 710 ms** (target ≤ 1.5 × one-GOP
  decode ≈ 1.1 s ✓; 19.7 pictures decoded per seek).
- **Phase 2 soak (synthetic): passed.** `bench --section xm5 --only soak` (new case,
  `FILMCRAFT_SOAK_MINUTES`), 120 min, 130 plays of 55 s: 214 314 shown / 3 dropped (builds ran
  meanwhile), RSS 187–227 MB without a trend (+12 MB net), VRAM +252 MB in the first 2 minutes
  (3 surfaces warming up) then flat, decode-cache VRAM flat, VA surfaces 67 → 70.
- **Multicut analysis:** every remaining drop (13/360) is the first 1–3 frames after a cut
  (`FILMCRAFT_BENCH_TRACE=1` lists late frames): the next clip is entered mid-GOP and needs up
  to ~29 pictures of catch-up (~0.5 s), but VCN decodes 6.2K at ~41 fps against 30 fps of
  playback, so only ~11 fps of spare capacity exist; requesting the next clip earlier (cut
  prefetch, or a deeper prefetch window: 20 → 35 drops, 24 → 52–75) runs both decodes on the
  one engine at once and makes things worse. A real fix schedules catch-up into spare capacity
  (decode in priority order / decode-ahead): **deferred to Phase 3.1** (decision: Phase 2's
  zero-copy goal is met; this is playback scheduling). Also tried without gain but kept (sound
  on their own): hardware decoders have their own live cap (8; software keeps the RAM-based
  cap), surface pools shared per format/size across decoders (4 pools, 8 spares).
- **Bug found by the full suite and fixed:** a dropped `FrameServer` never stopped its worker
  threads, which kept its caches (and the decoded frames / GPU surfaces in them) alive for the
  life of the process. Harmless in the app (one server) but every bench play makes a new one, so
  later cases in one process ran out of VRAM (VA surfaces 141 → 409, 12.5 GB) and the Phase 0
  bench's RSS growth (5.4 → 10 GB) had the same cause. Drop now stops the workers and empties
  the caches (`frames::tests::dropping_the_server_stops_its_workers`).
- **Phase 2 acceptance (synthetic, full suite, `docs/fork-benchmarks.md` "Phase 2"):** decode
  40.9 fps = 95 % of the ceiling ✓; play-full 1798/0 at 1.8 ms CPU/frame ✓; scrub p95 743 ms
  (≤ 1.1 s) ✓; 2-hour soak ✓; fallback ✓; **multicut 1477/303 ✗** — all drops right after cuts
  (see the analysis above). **Decision:** Phase 2 is closed with this exception, because its
  goal (in-process zero-copy decode → display) is met and the cut drops are playback
  scheduling; "`xm5-multicut` drops nothing" is added to Phase 3's acceptance.
- **Phase 2 committed (`baa98f0`, tag `phase-2`).** Build ready: `~/Apps/filmcraft-builds/phase-2/`

- **Next:** Phase 3 (Resolve-style playback): 3.1 decode-ahead with catch-up scheduled into spare
  decoder capacity (multicut 0 drops), 3.2 JKL (2× smooth, 4× graceful skipping, reverse from the
  GOP cache), 3.3 reduced resolution without CPU work on GPU frames (draft mode box-filters on the
  CPU → read-back today), 3.4 audio lock, 3.5 PipeWire.
- **Phase 3.1 work (2026-10-08, synthetic quick passes):**
  - **Bug found and fixed (correctness): open-GOP seeks.** A request for a leading (RASL) picture
    seeked to the CRA it follows; a decoder starting at a CRA drops its RASL pictures, so the
    frame was "not produced" after decoding 64 more pictures (~1.5 s), and playback showed a
    frame with a missing layer and counted it as shown. The GOP cache now starts from the sync
    sample before for leading pictures (`gop::tests::leading_pictures_of_an_open_gop_seek_from_the_gop_before`,
    fails without the fix). Consequence: every earlier `xm5-multicut` figure (13–24 drops) was
    flattered by that bug; with correct pictures it was ~98/360.
  - **Skipping unshown pictures at cuts:** a timeline clip never shows what precedes its
    in-point, so non-reference pictures before it are left out while catching up
    (`render::clip_frame`): multicut ~98 → **25–42 / 360**. Also fixed: `NalStreamInfo` took the
    highest TemporalId from `hvcC` `numTemporalLayers`, which ffmpeg's MOV muxer writes as 6 for a
    single-layer x265 stream, so no picture counted as disposable; the SPS's
    `sps_max_sub_layers_minus1` now bounds it.
  - **Decode priority gate** (`filmcraft_codecs::hwgate`: hardware decode calls in deadline order,
    deadlines from `FrameServer` jobs via `filmcraft_media::cancel::with_deadline`): kept, but no
    measurable gain (VA queues work asynchronously, so only submissions are ordered; waiting for
    each picture inside the gate cost ~6 % throughput — 41 → 38.5 fps — without fewer drops).
  - **Dead ends (measured):** starting the first frame after the next cut early (without the
    gate: 20–60 frames ahead → 86–215 drops; with the gate and correct pictures: 45 frames → 43–44,
    90 → 47–48, vs 25–42 without); a deeper prefetch window (20 → 35, 24 → 47–75, 30 → 67–78
    drops); a smaller per-clip floor (16 → ~85 drops before the eviction change); a smaller
    VRAM cache budget or different plan charges (no gain once VRAM was below the limit).
    Also changed: GOP-cache eviction now prefers frames outside the span of recent requests.
  - **Why zero drops at cuts is out of reach here:** cuts every 3 s into 6.2K open-GOP clips need
    ~90 shown frames plus up to ~30 catch-up pictures per clip ≈ 40 fps of decoding against a
    ~41 fps engine; even with skipping it stays at ~85–95 % of capacity. **Owner decision
    (2026-10-08): "zero drops at cuts" moves to Phase 6** (proxies / render cache decode less),
    with a task to measure realistic cut spacing.
- **Session 1 ends here (2026-10-08, owner's request: hand over to the runner script).** State:
  Phases 0–2 done and built; Phase 3 started (3.1 committed). §1a rewritten with every standing

  (§1a.5), then Phase 3.2 (JKL).**
- **2026-10-08, session 2: unsafe / FFI review (§1a.5), round 1 (committed).** An interrupted
  session had left review fixes uncommitted; checked, finished and committed: typed VA parameter
  buffers (`Context::param_buffer` for the three HEVC parameter structs, sealed trait) replace
  `va_hevc::as_bytes` (reading a padded struct as `&[u8]` is UB); `Buffer` borrows its `Context`
  (destroyed before it, cannot be submitted to another context); `Context` is `!Sync`; slice data
  bounds checked against the NAL (`slice_data_byte_offset ≤ size`); `Surface::read` checks the
  rectangle and uses checked row arithmetic; the DMA-BUF description owns its fds (`DmaBuf.owner`)
  so a clone can never outlive them; plane `object_size` per object; spare surfaces reused oldest
  first; Vulkan import requires Vulkan 1.1, checks the modifier / plane count / sampled feature /
  max extent / importability (`check_modifier`), the layer fourccs against the picture format, a
  non-negative fd and an owning description. Regression test
  `va_interop::hostile_dmabuf_descriptions_are_refused` (fd −1 panicked in `BorrowedFd::borrow_raw`
  before). Conformance still bit-exact (39/39), in-place == read-back (24 frames). Round 2: an
  independent reviewer agent's findings.

- **2026-10-08, session 3: unsafe / FFI review round 2 (committed).** The previous session had left
  round-2 fixes uncommitted; checked, finished (test updated to the new fields, a lint) and
  committed: `DmaBuf` owns its buffer objects (`objects: Arc<[OwnedFd]>`, planes name an object
  index) so a description can no longer carry a closed or foreign fd; only single-memory-plane
  layers are imported (metadata planes of compressed modifiers would need a foreign-queue
  acquire), the buffer's size comes from the kernel (seek to end of the DMA-BUF) instead of
  libva's report; Vulkan 1.1 required of the physical device too; HEVC parameters the driver
  trusts are refused when hostile: more than 15 references, reference-list entries past the
  filled slots, tile counts beyond VA's arrays or the picture, explicit tile sizes that don't
  fit (u32 overflow in the uniform split panicked in debug builds before:
  `va_hevc::tests::hostile_tile_sizes_are_refused`); read-back rectangle checked before
  allocating. Tests: conformance bit-exact 39/39, in-place == read-back 24/24,
  `hostile_dmabuf_descriptions_are_refused` (5 alterations), layers + wasm ✓. Owner decisions
  from `CLAUDE.md` recorded as §1a.14–15 (`307ea2c`). Next: round 3, a fresh independent pass
  over every remaining `unsafe` block, then the ADR 0002 record, then Phase 3.2.
- **2026-10-08, session 3: unsafe / FFI review round 3 — review done (§1a.5).** A fresh pass over
  every `unsafe` block (`va.rs`, `va_hevc.rs`, `va_sys.rs` loading, `interop.rs`). Fixed:
  `Context::decode` refuses a target surface of another display or smaller than the context (the
  driver would write past it; `va::tests::a_context_refuses_a_smaller_target_surface`, runs on
  the AMD device); `Surface::read` converts coordinates to libva's `int` with a check instead of
  `as`. Checked and kept: library loading, RAII of every libva object, fd ownership on all
  error paths, sync before hand-out, wgpu-hal ownership of imported image and memory. Residual
  risks accepted and written down: `UNDEFINED` → sampled layout on the imported image (contents
  kept for the uncompressed modifiers in use, tested every frame; a foreign-queue acquire is not
  exposed by wgpu) and a recycled surface overwritten while older GPU work may still sample it
  (wrong picture at worst, never freed memory). Full record: ADR 0002 "Review of the unsafe /
  FFI code". Tests: clippy ✓, platform tests incl. conformance 39/39 bit-exact ✓, `va_interop`
  ✓. Session stopped here at the owner's request. **Next: Phase 3.2 (JKL).**
- **2026-10-08, session 4: Phase 3.2 JKL (done, synthetic, `docs/fork-benchmarks.md` "Phase 3.2").**
  No leftover changes; no `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`. Before: L 2× 50/189, L 4× 2/238, J 1× 44/76,
  J 2× 38/202 (quick). Causes found and fixed:
  - **Above 1× playback asked for every frame**: the frame due moved one timeline frame per
    refresh, so prefetch (key + i·step) covered every frame and 2× needed 60 fps of decoding.
    Now the playhead shows a fixed **skimming grid** (`SkimControl`, `FrameServer::playback_frame`):
    every `step`-th frame, starting at the speed's natural step, climbing a ladder (2, 3, 4, 5, 6,
    8, 10, 12, 15, 20, …) when 3 grid frames in a row are late (two rungs when 6 of 8 are), back
    down after 48 calm frames with frames ahead ready (doubled each time a step down had to be
    undone), remembered per speed. Monitor, multicam, meter and bench use the same grid.
  - **Skimming decode** (`cancel::with_skim`, `GopCache::frame_skim`): any picture within half
    a step will do. Every non-reference picture except the target is left out; a non-reference
    target is replaced by a reference neighbour; a sync sample within the tolerance is preferred
    (a leading picture of an open GOP otherwise costs the whole GOP before it); a later GOP is
    reached by a seek; a frame the decoder already passed (another worker asked for a later one
    first) gets the nearest decoded frame within a grid step instead of a seek back; the target
    is flushed out when the next grid frame lies in another GOP. Off-grid jobs are dropped when
    the step changes.
  - **Reverse**: a cache hit waited for the decoder lock while another job decoded the GOP
    before (≈1 s): decoded frames now have their own lock (`Store`), hits never wait. The cache
    keeps a GOP's worth while playing backwards (detected from the requests), evicts frames
    already handed out first, and one run decodes the whole display range (through the next sync
    sample and its leading pictures), flushed at its end. Reverse prefetch 40 frames, preroll 30
    frames / 2 s. The plan cache evicts frames playback has passed first (LRU evicted the
    prefetched frames still to be shown).
  - **GPU stall while the video engine runs flat out (driver behaviour, measured):** decoding a
    GOP back to back made the compositing pass wait 40–100 ms per frame (composite 0.4 ms, the
    rest `device.poll`), ~25 drops in 300; forward playback (spread decoding) never showed it.
    Not the DMA-BUF import (same with `FILMCRAFT_NO_GPU_IMPORT`), not surface churn (48 spare
    surfaces: no change), not the VA queue depth (1, 2, 3, 64 in flight: 12–44 drops). Pacing
    the hardware decoder fixed it: reverse playback paces submissions to 1.25 pictures per frame
    interval (`cancel::with_pace`, `REVERSE_PACE`). Root cause in Mesa/amdgpu not identified.
  - Results (10 s per case): **L 2× 299/1** (a new picture every 33 ms), **J 1× 296/4** (the 4
    at start-up; steady state 0), L 4× warm 95/10 (step 10–12, new picture p50 100 / max 268 ms),
    J 2× warm 70/10 (step 6–8, max 300 ms). Cold 4× / J 2× climb for ~2 s (max gap 0.7 s). On
    this stream (51 % reference pictures, open GOP) 4× costs ~40 pictures/s even at step 12–15,
    i.e. it runs at the decoder's limit: steady skipping, not more. VRAM peak 10.4 GB (J 2×).
    No regression in play-full / multicut / scrub (A/B against `08e41d6`).
  - Tests: `gop::tests::{skimming_shows_reference_pictures_and_leaves_the_others_out,
    skimming_prefers_a_sync_sample_and_never_seeks_back, playing_backwards_decodes_each_gop_once}`,
    `frames::tests::{skimming_*, reverse_playback_prefetches_more_than_a_gop,

- **2026-10-08, session 4: Phase 4.2b part 1 — input transforms on the GPU (done, synthetic).**
  A Y'CbCr (or GPU-surface) clip that needs colour management in a Rec. 709 sequence (log, HDR,
  wide gamut, any Interpret Footage override) is no longer a CPU layer: the plan hands the GPU
  its signal (the frame with a linear transfer: Y'CbCr → R'G'B' only, unclamped, shared pixels)
  and the input transform as the first `FxOp::ColorChain` (`transform::input_ops`: Clamp + the
  decode table over `DecodeTable::for_curve`'s range, HLG OOTF, gamut matrix, BT.2390 tone map,

  `DesaturateIntoGamut`, `Clamp`, all with GPU parity ≤ 1e-4). Such a layer is converted at the
  source's full size (averaging its signal before the log decode would not average light). Also
  fixed: a frame with a linear transfer was clamped to 0…1 and quantised to 12 bits on the CPU.
  **`xm5-play-flog2`** (new case: the 6.2K clip interpreted as F-Log2, Full, quick): **0/240 at
  246.7 ms CPU/frame, RSS 3.5 GB → 240/0 at 3.4 ms, RSS 0.2 GB** (GPU 54 % busy). Tests:
  `input_ops_match_the_input_transform` (every colour space × range × 3 pipelines vs
  `InputTransform`), `log_media_plans_its_input_transform_on_the_gpu` (plan vs CPU render:
  ≤ 2e-3 at full scale; at 1/2 the reference averages before tone mapping, the plan after),
  GPU parity for every input transform. Next: part 2, the DWG / DI working space and the output
  transform on the GPU (non-Rec. 709 sequences still composite on the CPU).
- **2026-10-08, session 4: Phase 4.2b part 2 — DaVinci Wide Gamut / Intermediate working space,
  output transform on the GPU (done, synthetic).** `WorkingSpace::DaVinciWide` (id `dwg-di`):
  scene-linear DWG compositing (log media enters without tone mapping), Lumetri grades the DI
  signal (`GradeSpace::DaVinciIntermediate`), the output transform tone maps from the log peak
  (4000 cd/m², as log → Rec. 709 does) and maps to Rec. 709; exports stay SDR Rec. 709.

  sequences (DWG, HDR, wide gamut) no longer fall back to whole-frame CPU rendering for the

  `Saturation` gained `vibrance`; `HlgOotf` gives black for no light (the inverse OOTF's negative
  exponent). `GradeSpace::{encode_ops, decode_ops}` (SDR, PQ, HLG, DI as ops; test against
  `encode` / `decode`). `effects::lumetri_grade` builds Basic (without Input LUT) + Creative
  (without Look) + Vignette as one chain; the CPU Lumetri runs it; `lumetri_gpu` hands it to the

  Intel): new ops ≤ 3e-7, whole grades ≤ 4.4e-5 (PQ). Gates: fmt, clippy, layers, wasm,
  workspace tests (release) ✓. **Next: 4.3b** Curves + Colour Wheels, Input LUT / Look as
  operations (3D LUT op, tetrahedral as `lut.rs`).
- **2026-10-09, session 5: Phase 4.3b Curves, Colour Wheels, Look and LUTs as colour operations
  (done).** New `ColorOp`s: `Curve` (per-channel tone curve, HDR slope-1 extension), `Wheels`,
  `HslCurves` (hue vs hue / sat / luma, luma vs sat, sat vs sat), `Lut` (1D shaper + tetrahedral

  Oracle (`lumetri_chain_tests`, old `Advanced` code kept `cfg(test)`): ≤ 2e-4, PQ ≤ 1e-3 (PQ's

  whole grades ≤ 7.7e-5. Gates + workspace tests (release) ✓. **Next: 4.3c** HSL Secondary
  on the GPU: key pass (chain writing a mask), median + box blur on the mask, correction chain
  reading the mask (ops get a mask register).
- **2026-10-09, session 5: Phase 4.3c HSL Secondary on the GPU (done, synthetic).** Colour
  operations got a mask register (`ColorOp::apply_px`, `apply_chain_px`); new ops `HslKey`

- **2026-10-09, session 5: Phase 4.5 reframing (done).** **Maximum Render Quality** (sequence
  setting, already stored but unused by the renderer; export's "Use Maximum Render Quality")
  now resamples scaled-down clips through a **Lanczos-3** filter scaled to the footprint
  (`Image::transformed_hq` on the CPU, `layer_color_hq` in `composite.wgsl`; taps on texel
  centres up to a footprint of 2, else 13 bilinear taps per axis; normalised, alpha clamped;
  edges repeat like the GPU's quad). Measured at 6.2K → 4K (×0.615): passband (0.7 Nyquist)
  92 % contrast kept; beyond Nyquist 0.004 vs bilinear's 0.217 (≈ 55× less aliasing). GPU vs
  CPU (AMD + Intel): 8-bit max 1. Used by exports with Maximum Render Quality (decoded at full
  size, `RenderOptions::high_quality`) and by the Program monitor **while paused**; playback
  keeps the box supersampling (decision (agent): the graded 6.2K playback already uses 87 % of
  the GPU; a 100-tap filter per pixel would cost drops). **Sequence presets**
  (`file.newSequence {preset}`, list `sequence.presets`): UHD 3840×2160 and vertical 2160×3840
  at 23.976 / 29.97 in Rec. 709, the same as `xm5-*` in DaVinci Wide Gamut, and X-M5 6.2K
  masters; explicit width / height / fps override a preset (they no longer default in the
  schema, so a form sending defaults cannot undo a preset). Fill / Fit per clip already existed

  gamut. Test `crates/color/tests/fujifilm_lut.rs` (skips without the file): **tone (greys)
  matches to 0.054 of a 10-bit code**; colours do not: Fujifilm's LUT applies the gamut matrix
  to the **log signal** (that model fits it to a mean 9.5 / max 44 codes), while the built-in
  transform converts in linear light (mean 55 / max 453 codes from the LUT on saturated colours).
  **Decision (agent):** keep the linear-light conversion (correct colour science, as Resolve's
  colour management); the official `.cube` remains available as an Input LUT (now on the GPU)
  for Fujifilm's rendering. The acceptance criterion "matches the official LUT within a small
  tolerance" is met for tone, not for saturated colours, for the reason above.

  scripts pass it) was silently ignored, so **every "Main 10" export benchmark since Phase 0 was
  8-bit**; now text or number (test `hevc_bit_depth_accepts_text`). (2) The CPU export path wrote
  one black frame (frame 63 of 90) in that comparison; the GPU path did not (pre-existing; noted
  for Phase 7 robustness). (3) The headless device asks for the adapter's buffer limits (a 6.2K
  frame's planes are ~156 MB). **Results (60 s, 10-bit for real):** export 4K **16.5 fps**
  (Phase 4: 4.9 fps, 8-bit), graded 4K **13.0 fps** (0.6), 6.2K **8.2 fps** (4.0)
  (`target/bench/xm5-5.1-export-10bit.json`). Gates + workspace tests ✓. **Next: 5.2** overlap
  rendering and encoding (the job renders a batch, then encodes it), then in-process VA-API
  encode from GPU frames.
- **2026-10-09, session 5: Phase 5.2a render / encode overlap (done, synthetic).** The export job
  renders a batch (12 frames here) and then hands it to the encoder, whose ffmpeg writer queue
  held 3 frames, so the GPU waited for ffmpeg to take 9. The queue now holds up to 16 frames
  within 512 MB (`hevc_export::write_queue`: 16 at 4K 10-bit, 6 at 6.2K). 60 s, 10-bit
  (`target/bench/xm5-5.2-export.json`, load ≈ 5): **4K 19.0 fps** (16.5), graded 13.6 (13.0),
  6.2K 9.3 (8.2). Analysis: graded export is GPU-bound by the full-size 6.2K grade (the F-Log2
  input transform + Lumetri run on the 6.2K working image, ~29 ms of GPU per frame at 86 % busy
  in playback); ungraded 4K by decode + composite + read-back + ffmpeg's upload/encode in series.
  **Next: 5.2b** in-process VA-API HEVC encode from the GPU picture (no read-back, no pipe), then
  presets / ffprobe checks.

- **2026-10-09, session 6: Phase 5.2b in-process VA-API HEVC encode (done, synthetic).**
  `va_sys.rs` regenerated with `va_enc_hevc.h` and the encode types by a committed script
  (`crates/platform/gen-va-sys.sh`; the HANDOFF's "command in this file" did not exist: the
  original allowlist was reconstructed and reproduces the old file byte for byte without the
  encode types). `filmcraft_hevc::write`: VPS / SPS / PPS / slice headers (I/P, one ref, one
  slice, VUI colour + timing, level / tier from Table A.8), tested by parsing them back with our
  own parser. `va.rs`: `Config::with_attribs`, `Display::config_attribs`, packed headers, misc
  parameters (`Misc<T>`), `CodedBuffer` (owned, bounded segment walk, overflow → error),
  `Context::encode_hevc` (fills surface / coded-buffer ids itself after checks), `Surface::write`
  (vaCreateImage + vaPutImage). `va_enc.rs` (safe): `VaHevcEncoder`, CQP / CBR / VBR, IDR every
  keyint, 3 pictures in flight, driver block sizes / features honoured; decodes in FilmCraft's
  software decoder at > 40 dB (QP 20). `hevc_export`: backend order **VA-API in process → ffmpeg
  `hevc_vaapi` → `libx265`** (`Backend::VaNative`, `FILMCRAFT_NO_VA_ENCODE` turns it off); the
  encoder runs on its own thread behind a queue; mid-stream failure → the unfinished frames go
  to ffmpeg (libx265 without B-frames) from an IDR (test injects a failure at frame 11; every
  frame once, in order). Encoder alone at 4K 10-bit: 11.8 ms/frame incl. upload (~85 fps).
  **Found:** export time was not the encoder: `read_yuv420` (GPU → CPU) took 28 ms per 4K frame
  under the compositor lock (two fresh 50 MB buffers per frame, one u32 per sample, single-thread
  CPU repack). Now a GPU pack pass writes `yuv420p10le` / `yuv420p` bytes and the buffers are
  reused: 11 ms (4K), parity tests unchanged on AMD + Intel. **Results (60 s, 10-bit, synthetic;
  two runs, the second with swap activity):** export 4K **23.7 / 19.9 fps** (5.2a: 19.0), graded
  15.4 / 14.6 (13.6), 6.2K 12.3 / 13.2 (9.3) (`target/bench/xm5-5.2b-export.json` = first run of
  the encoder alone: 20.3 / 13.5 / 9.5). Per-frame trace of the 4K case: batch render 35 ms
  (decode-bound ≈ 24 ms + the batch's tail), encode stage 6 ms. Gates ✓ (fmt, clippy, tests of
  hevc / platform / gpu / export, layers, wasm). **Next:** render the next batch while the
  current one encodes (`Exporter::step`), then 5.2c zero-copy input, 5.3 graded speed.
- **2026-10-09, session 6: Phase 5.2b-2 batch overlap (done, synthetic).** `Exporter::step` renders
  the next batch on a scoped thread while the current one is encoded and muxed (`pipe` is an
  `Arc<Pipeline>`, `ahead` holds the rendered batch; a batch whose range changed is rendered
  again; not on the web, where a pending source re-renders a batch before it is encoded). 60 s,
  10-bit (`target/bench/xm5-5.2b-overlap.json`, load ≈ 6): **4K 24.8 fps** (≥ 24 = 80 % of the
  29.9 fps ceiling ✓), graded 15.8, **6.2K 14.7** (9.3 at 5.2a). Gates ✓ (fmt, clippy, export +
  engine tests, layers, wasm). **Next:** 5.3 graded export ≥ 30 fps (the grade runs on the 6.2K
  source size: ~36 ms of GPU per frame), 5.2c zero-copy input.
- **2026-10-09, session 6: Phase 5.3a converted media graded at the size drawn (done,
  synthetic).** A log / wide-gamut clip (input transform on the GPU) drawn below 95 % of its
  size is now graded on a working image of the size drawn instead of the full 6.2K
  (`plan.rs`; `LayerFx.size` smaller than the decimated frame = resample; GPU
  `fx_source_uniforms` with Maximum Render Quality's filter, CPU `effect_image` bilinear; parity
  test `working_image_smaller_than_the_frame_matches_cpu`, mean diff 4e-5, AMD + Intel). Like
  Resolve's timeline-resolution processing, the signal is resampled before the input transform;
  this overrides session 5's "converted frames at full size" (agent decision: averaging log
  signal differs from averaging light only at sharp edges between very different levels).
  `FILMCRAFT_FULL_SIZE_GRADE=1` keeps the full size. Applies to playback too (cheaper grading
  in the monitors). Check: 5 s graded 4K export, scaled vs full-size grade: 36.7 dB luma PSNR,
  mean luma within 1 code (of 1023), visually the same (the difference is the synthetic noise
  sharpened at another scale). Ungraded exports are bit-identical run to run. **Results (60 s,
  `target/bench/xm5-5.3-export.json`):** graded 4K **21.5 fps** (15.8), 4K 25.2, 6.2K 14.9.
  Experiments: VA encode quality levels (1, 2, 4, 8) change nothing; decoding with the encoder
  looping flat out at ~100 fps 4K drops 41.1 → 35.5 fps (decode and encode share the one VCN;
  not the limit at 30 fps). Bench: `FILMCRAFT_BENCH_KEEP=<dir>` keeps each export's output.
  **Next:** find why export decodes at ~29 fps when the decode bench does 41 (export trace:
  threads wait on decoded frames).
- **2026-10-09, session 6: Phase 5.4 presets and ffprobe checks (done).** New presets (H.265 /
  HEVC group): **YouTube 4K HEVC** (3840×2160 Main 10, VBR 80 / 100 Mbps, AAC), **6.2K Master
  HEVC** (6240×4160 Main 10, VBR 200 / 240 Mbps, QuickTime, 24-bit PCM), **Archive HEVC (x265,
  quality)** (libx265 slow, CRF 18, Main 10, QuickTime + PCM), **Vertical 2160×3840 HEVC**
  (scale to fill, VBR 60 / 75 Mbps); ProRes 422 HQ already existed. `ExportSettings.x265_preset`
  (`x265Preset` param, checked against `X265_PRESETS`; ffmpeg was always `medium`). Test
  `crates/ui-egui/tests/hevc_presets.rs` exports each with the platform encoder registered:
  ffprobe confirms container, HEVC Main 10, yuv420p10le, size, 24000/1001, primaries / transfer
  / matrix bt709 and range tv, audio codec; mpv decodes every file without errors. ✓
  Owner asked (session 6) why graded 4K is slower than Resolve when the GPU is not the
  bottleneck: answered from the measurements: GPU ~11–15 ms of 33 ms, decoder 41 fps (35 with
  the encoder running), so the loss is our pipeline (read-back, CPU copy, re-upload, one frame
  at a time behind a blocking GPU wait); fix = 5.2c zero-copy + frames in flight. **Next: 5.2c.**
- **2026-10-10, session 7: Phase 5.2c zero-copy export (done, synthetic).** The compositor writes
  P010 / NV12 straight into the in-process encoder's input surfaces (`Surface::export_writable`,
  `interop::Importer::import_target` as UNORM render targets — ANV lists no Y-tiled modifier for
  `R16_UINT` targets; `yuv.rs` `run_into` renders the samples of the existing 4:2:0 pass;
  `VaHevcEncoder::encode_surface`). No read-back, CPU copy or `vaPutImage`; render threads no
  longer wait for the GPU (the `VaPicture` carries its submission and its plan; the encoder thread
  waits). Fallback: read-back path (driver can't render into the surfaces, checked once under an
  error scope; non-VA encoder; mid-stream failure reads pending GPU frames back for ffmpeg);
  `FILMCRAFT_NO_ZERO_COPY=1` forces it. **Parity:** surfaces byte-identical to the read-back path
  and identical bitstreams, AMD and Intel (`gpu_export` test); real 5 s exports decode
  identically run to run and to the read-back path. **Bug found by that check (pre-existing):**
  the GOP cache could evict a frame as it stored it (parallel workers had moved its request span
  past it) → "frame not produced" → a black frame in the export (seen ~1 run in 3); fixed, with a
  regression test. **Results (60 s, before the cache fix, load ≈ 6, firefox open;
  `target/bench/xm5-5.2c-export.json`):** 4K 23.3 fps, graded 22.9 (21.5), 6.2K 18.2 (14.9).
  **Finding (`FILMCRAFT_EXPORT_TRACE=1`):** rendering is no longer the limit; the VA encoder is:
  ~35 ms per 4K frame in the export vs 10.5–12.7 ms alone (changing noise frames). With the
  decoder flat out the AMD encoder takes ~54 ms/frame: Navi22 has **one VCN**, shared by the
  6.2K decode (24.4 ms/frame alone) and the encode (~8–10 ms) → ≈ 31 fps is the single-engine
  ceiling, ≥ 30 graded can't be met reliably on it. The Intel UHD 750's own media engine encodes
  4K 10-bit at 27 ms/frame incl. upload (~37 fps) in parallel. **Next:** 5.2d encode on the
  second GPU's media engine when the decoding GPU's engine is shared (agent decision).
- **2026-10-10, session 7: 5.2d measurements (synthetic).** `FILMCRAFT_VAAPI_ENCODE_DEVICE` moves
  only the HEVC encoder to another render node (`Display::for_encode`). Encode on the Intel UHD
  750 while AMD decodes (read-back path, zero-copy can't cross devices): **4K 18.5 fps, graded
  19.5, 6.2K 9.9** (quick) — worse than AMD alone. Encoder pictures in flight 3 vs 10: no
  difference (60 s graded 4K: 24.0 / 24.1, 26.1 / 26.7). Export decodes each frame once (149
  frames: 151 decoded, 1 seek; bench prints GOP counters now; `--only` selects one export case).
  Encoder alone at 6240×4160 10-bit: AMD 30.7 ms/frame, Intel 72.9 ms. With the 6.2K decode at
  24.4 ms on the same VCN, **6.2K export's ceiling on this card is ≈ 18–20 fps** (measured 18.2–
  18.9); graded 4K's is ≈ 31 fps (measured 24–28.4). Owner asked (session 7) about 6.2K ≥ 24 fps:
  answered from these numbers (only a split of the encode between AMD and Intel could reach it,
  on paper ~24–27 fps, uncertain).

  |---|---|---|---|---|

  | 5 s | 36 / 34 | 20 / 13 | 4–5 of 12 | first 15 frames |

- **2026-10-12 01:00, 6.5 done: root cause of the cut drops found and fixed (`9b4bfe66`).** Fable
  diagnostician from the per-cut traces: the GOP cache's `CONTINUE_THROUGH` (48 samples,
  `crates/codecs/src/gop.rs`) kept a clip's running decoder going forward on a cut back into the
  same clip, although every picture before the new in-point was late: ~30–40 unwanted reference
  pictures per re-entered cut (multicut re-enters each clip 2.3 s past where it left it; the 3 s
  spacing pattern only 0.7 s, hence the difference). It predicted the GOP seek count (14) and the
  bad cuts exactly. Fix: continue only when `late_before` is before the sync sample (sequential

- **2026-10-12 05:17, 6.1b DMA-BUF hand-off Intel → AMD merged** (`f6d5230c`; Opus lane, Opus
  FFI review → fixes `30aca650`). iHD always tiles Y210, so the linear memory comes from Vulkan
  (`interop::alloc_dmabuf`: host-visible + host-coherent, never a discrete GPU's device-local
  memory, external-buffer query first, dedicated allocation when required), imported by iHD as a
  DRM PRIME 2 Y210 surface, VPP copy, sync, export; AMD imports it as Rgba16Unorm and a compute
  pass (`gpu/src/unpack422.rs`) unpacks to R16 / RG16 planes. CPU read-back maps the linear
  DMA-BUF (`DMA_BUF_IOCTL_SYNC`) so the decoded surface goes back to the pool right after the
  copy. Fallbacks: env `FILMCRAFT_NO_GPU_IMPORT`, no VPP / linear surface / failed copy or export
  → read-back per stream; a compositor refusal sets a process-wide flag → all streams read back
  (logged once). Parity: 18 4:2:2 fixtures + 6.2K xm5 first pictures, AMD and Intel compositing;
  pool-reuse stress test. Timings (release, 6240×4160, synthetic, other lane building): decode →
  GPU frame **44–47 ms hand-off vs 103–146 ms read-back + upload** (~21–22 fps). Post-merge:
  platform + gpu + frame 183 passed (AMD + Intel), clippy, fmt, wasm green. Owed: decode-to-
  screen fps at 1× / 2× in the app; Dell check; the VPP context is leaked once per display on
  purpose (destroying it crashed iHD's vaSyncSurface).

  SPS / PPS verbatim, but **radeonsi rewrites the SPS / PPS from its own parameters**
  (max_transform_hierarchy_depth inter / intra 4 where we send the attribute's value,
  dependent_slice_segments_enabled 1, init_qp 26), so AMD and Intel chunks do **not** share one
  `hvcC` today; each chunk decodes with its own entry. S8 options: (a) `hev1` with in-band sets
  (the design's fallback, always correct), (b) make the shared stream match radeonsi's rewrite
  (depths 4, dependent slices flag, init_qp 26) if iHD accepts those values in its VA buffers too,
  then check the actual bitstream headers, not `headers()`. Recommended: try (b), fall back to (a).
  platform tests all green (77 lib), clippy clean.

## HANDOFF notes of U0.2 (archived 2026-10-13 at the `upstream-sync-2` tag)

### Exact next step (as it was): U0.2 (UPSTREAM-PLAN U0 stage 2: one VA-API stack), step U0.2.5

1. **Weekly upstream sync** is due from 2026-10-18 (last: Sync 1, 2026-10-11; upstream had 565 new
   commits on 2026-10-13, still none in `vaapi/`). Re-check `vaapi/` drift before each U0.2 step.
2. **U0.2.1 done (Y1). U0.2.2 done (2026-10-13, merge `18fddc60`):** HEVC Main / Main10 4:2:0 goes
   through upstream's `vaapi/hevc.rs::Front` with `ZeroCopyAccel` first in `vaapi_factory`
   (`NAME_HEVC_ZERO_COPY`); `va_hevc::NativeVaDecoder` only when `VaDecoder::front_takes` refuses
   (4:2:2, range extensions, Rext 4:2:0 intra profiles). Grey stand-ins for missing references on
   the zero-copy path only (`Front::set_conceal_missing`); hostile tile layouts (`tile_sizes`) and
   entry points / zero-data slices refused in the front end; `ZeroCopyAccel` decodes on the
   thread's decode node (6E lanes) and frames keep the context alive (`Arc<Mutex<Context>>`: fixes
   an iHD `vaSyncSurface` SIGSEGV after the decoder was dropped, H.264 too). Gates (synthetic):
   conformance parity AMD + Intel (`va_native` 37 fixtures via the front end, 22 via the fork
   decoder), `vaapi.rs` 20/20 both GPUs; xm5 quick: play-full 240/0 1.96 ms, graded 240/0 3.41 ms
   (= U0); export quick dual A/B vs a base binary (`target/fork-bench/bench-phase6e` vs
   `bench-u022`): 22.1 / 26.2 / 17.2 fps vs 22.2 / 11.0 / 17.2 (graded quick dual is very noisy);
   multicut A/B drops 50, 49 vs 60, 22 (noise) **but CPU per frame 2.9–3.2 vs 2.2–2.5 ms and ~8 %
   fewer frames due**: look at it in U0.2.3 (suspect: CPU grey fills for RASL / missing refs at
   open-GOP entries, 78 MB per picture at 6.2K, review finding 4: reuse the grey surface).

      **Diagnosis so far (2026-10-13, quick runs, bench `target/fork-bench/bench-u023b-c`):**
      4K graded (Auto → AMD only) 21.3 / 20.6 fps; 4K ungraded forced single
      (`FILMCRAFT_BENCH_ENGINES=single`) 27.7 / 28.8 fps. Difference ≈ 12 ms per frame ≈ the AMD GPU

      python `PR_SET_PTRACER` parent, see memory `fork-lessons`): export workers wait on the clip's

      `drmSyncobjWait`). Lumetri op evaluation is 0.02 ms (not it). **Pitfall:**
      `FILMCRAFT_EXPORT_GPU_PROBE` waits for the GPU while holding the compositor lock: it inflates
      "gpu lock wait"; don't combine it with throughput numbers. **Correction (full length,
      single GPU, `FILMCRAFT_BENCH_ENGINES=single`, bench `bench-u023b-d`):** 4K ungraded 23.3 fps,
      4K graded 23.1 / 22.0 fps: **grading costs nothing on one AMD lane**; the quick-run gap was
      noise (quick graded runs swung 18 → 27 fps). One lane is decode-bound: new trace counter
      "va decode sync" (`trace::DEC_SYNC`, around `ZeroCopyAccel::read`'s sync) ≈ 70 s of 77 s
      wall, and it decodes 2400 pictures for 1798 frames (GOP seeks 22, evicted 2127: ~33 % decoded
      twice). So d = a faster single lane. The re-decodes are mostly inherent: 22 GOP seeks × ~27
      pictures ≈ the ~600 extra (cuts entering clips mid-GOP decode from the GOP start), so not a
      cache bug. **Tried: two chunk lanes both on the RX 6700 XT** (diagnostic
      `FILMCRAFT_EXPORT_SECOND_NODE=/dev/dri/renderD129` + `FILMCRAFT_BENCH_ENGINES=dual`, bench

      22.3 (lane 2 took no chunks). **Reading: AMD alone is VCN-bound** — one media engine does both
      decode (~29 ms sync per picture, 2400 pictures) and encode (~28 ms per frame), ≈ the 77 s wall;

      decodes (69.7 fps at 6.2K vs AMD 35.4), AMD composites and encodes (Intel encodes at only 18 fps).

- **2026-10-13 EDT, side lane (worktree): UI test binary memory fixed.** `cargo test -p filmcraft-ui-egui
  --test ui -- --test-threads=1` grew ~0.5–1 GB per test (Dell: OOM at ~12 GB in `coach_ui`; this PC:
  8 GB after 16 tests). Not a static leak (idle RSS returns to ~0.3 GB with a fixed glibc mmap
  threshold): each test's dropped app left its work running for 5–15 s (the render cache's 1080p demo
  batches and in-flight frame jobs on the shared rayon pool, each holding its frames, generator caches
  and media pool), and that work starved behind the next test's, so it piled up. Fixes: the background
  worker cancels its running job on shutdown and has `stop_and_wait`; `render_batch` and the batch
  rendered ahead carry the job's cancel flag into rayon (`filmcraft_media::cancel::current`) and skip
  frames not started; `encode_batch` stops between frames; `FrameServer::stop_and_wait`; **`impl Drop
  for FilmcraftApp`** stops and waits (≤ 3 s each) for both. Result: the whole UI binary in one process, `--test-threads=1`: **371 passed, peak
  2.88 GB, ~1.3–1.9 GB flat to the end** ; after merging hevc-vaapi 54795eb3: 380 passed, flat ~1.6–1.9 GB
  except inside `hevc_presets`, which peaks at 3–7 GB by itself: its 6.2K preset runs as a 6E.3
  dual-lane export when two GPUs and ≥ 6 GB `MemAvailable` are there, so the peak follows free RAM;
  released right after. Worth a look in 6E acceptance: ~7 GB RSS for a 6-frame 6.2K dual export. Regression tests: `teardown_ui` (fails
  without the fix: 20 media-pool references survived the drop), background / frame-server / export
  cancel tests. **Dead end:** making demo generator frames abort on the frame job's cancel flag made
  the parallel UI run crawl (137 tests in 34 min; likely superseded requests never finishing, so
  nothing got cached); reverted: the closing app waits for the frame in hand instead.
  **Not fixed (pre-existing, same on 94c32319):** the UI binary in parallel mode (default 20 threads)
  needs ~1 GB per concurrent test (18 GB cap hit after 36–52 tests here); on 16 GB, run it with
  `--test-threads` ≤ 8 or 1.

## HANDOFF notes of AI1 (archived 2026-10-14 at the ai-1 tag)

### Exact next step (as of the ai-1 tag): AI1 (AI-PLAN.md, portable fast inference), step AI1.1

U0.2 is closed (tag `upstream-sync-2`; its working notes are archived at the end of
[`PROGRESS-LOG-ARCHIVE.md`](PROGRESS-LOG-ARCHIVE.md), "HANDOFF notes of U0.2"). Next phase per
§1a.6 / §1a.69: **AI1** (≈ 8–15 h), then **X422**, then Y3.

1. **AI1-PREP done** (2026-10-13): read [`PREP-AI1.md`](PREP-AI1.md) first. Key finding: candle's
   quantized matmul never uses `gemm` (batch-1 decode is a per-row quantized `vec_dot`), so the hot
   path is our own Q8_0 / Q4_K / Q6_K dots with run-time dispatch through `pulp` (already in
   Cargo.lock via gemm); `gemm` only for prefill f32 attention. Start by measuring one Q8_0 row loop
   (pulp, no flag) against candle-with-flag before porting the rest.
2. **AI1.1a done** (`crates/llm/src/quant.rs`, 2026-10-13): Q8_0 parse / quantize / dequantize /
   dot / matvec in safe Rust, dispatched by `pulp` through `WithSimd` structs with `#[inline(always)]`
   (a plain closure is *not* vectorized: measured no gain), `SimdLevel::{Auto, Baseline}` +
   `FILMCRAFT_LLM_SIMD=baseline` override; all levels bit-identical (tested). Synthetic, 960 × 2560
   matvec, 1 thread: built **without** the flag ours 147 µs (baseline 202), candle 1 342 µs; with the
   flag candle 110 µs. **AI1.1b done:** rayon row split
   (64 rows / task, bit-identical to one thread); 12 threads: no-flag ours 41.8 µs, candle no-flag 332,
   candle with flag 41.7 → parity reached when threaded (a 4-rows-per-pass kernel gave nothing;
   the 1-thread 1.34× gap is the i8 multiply shape, candle's `vpmaddubsw`; optional later).
   **AI1.1c done** (`crates/llm/src/kquant.rs`): Q4_K / Q6_K parse (6-bit scales unpacked at load,
   weights stay packed), dequantize (bit-equal to candle's on random blocks), Q8_K quantize, dots /
   threaded matvecs via a zero-sized `Kernel` type (a `fn` pointer blocked inlining: measured).
   Synthetic 1024 × 2560, 12 threads, no flag: Q4_K 44.5 µs / Q6_K 52.4 µs vs candle no-flag 139 / 149,
   candle with flag 31 / 37 (we are ~1.4× behind it; optional tuning later).
   **AI1.1d-1 done:** `gguf.rs` now keeps the tensor directory (`tensor_infos`, `tensor()`) and
   finite float metadata (`floats`, `float()`); `weights.rs` `Weight::read` (F32 / F16 / Q8_0 / Q4_K /
   Q6_K, dims checked, NaN F32 refused) with `row`, `vector`, `matvec` + `Scratch` (deliberately no
   activation cache: a pointer-keyed cache goes stale on in-place buffers; quantizing is ~1 %).
   **AI1.1d-2 done:** `forward.rs` (`Config::from_gguf`, `Forward::load / forward(&[u32], pos) /
   reset`): both families, matches candle on a synthetic tiny GGUF (both archs) and on the real
   SmolLM2-360M tune (steps 0–1 bit-identical, later steps same top token, ≤ 8 %: attention-sum
   order × 8-bit activation rounding). Synthetic timing, 10 tokens: **20 ms / token without the
   flag** (candle no-flag 74, flagged 36, both incl. load). Prefill is still token-by-token.
   **AI1.1e done:** Q5_0 widened exactly to Q8_0 at load (`quant::parse_q5_0_as_q8_0`); K-quant
   kernels now keep the reference's 8-lane float order, `rms_norm` divides and attention divides by
   √d as candle does → **step 0 bit-identical to candle's scalar path on all six local models**
   (Q8_0 models and Qwen 0.5B Q4_K_M even through step 1; later steps differ by attention-sum order
   × 8-bit rounding, worst SmolLM2-1.7B ~15 %; the fact checker is the gate). Synthetic, 10 tokens,
   no flag: 20–26 ms / token (360M / 0.5B), 69–76 ms (1.5B / 1.7B), all faster than candle no-flag.
   Real-model check: `FILMCRAFT_LLM_GGUF=<file> RUSTFLAGS="-C target-cpu=x86-64" … test --release
   -p filmcraft-llm --features infer --lib real_model -- --ignored --nocapture`.
   **Owed (optional, AI1.1g):** the exact-order Q4_K kernel vectorizes badly with AVX2 (1 thread
   786 µs vs 228 for the old inexact one; 12 threads 120 vs 44.5 µs, candle flagged 31); only the
   1.5B / 1.7B options lean on Q4_K.
   **AI1.1f-1 done:** one generic row kernel (`quant::rows_at<K: Kernel>`, weights × t activation
   rows; matvec = t 1) + `matmul_q8_0 / _q4k / _q6k`, `Weight::matmul`, batched `Forward::chunk`
   (64-token prompt chunks, attention parallel over tokens), all bit-identical to token-by-token
   (tested across a chunk boundary). Synthetic 300-token prefill, SmolLM2-360M tune: **ours 1.86 s
   without the flag** vs candle 15.9 s no-flag / 2.75 s flagged. Prefill is now compute-bound on
   the Q8_0 dot (~17 GMAC/s per thread; `vpmaddubsw`-shaped kernel would be the next big win,
   AI1.1g with the Q4_K item).
   **AI1.1f-2 done:** `sample.rs`: greedy (first max, = candle's argmax) and repeat penalty equal
   to candle's (tested against it); temperature sampling with our own seeded splitmix64 (not
   candle's random sequence; greedy is the default and the evaluated setting).
   **AI1.1h done:** `infer.rs` now runs on `Forward` + `Sampler` (public API unchanged; candle is a
   dev-dependency: test oracle + `llm_quantize`). Real models, no flag (`tests/model.rs`, synthetic
   prompts): tuned smol360-v1 **1.57 s**, qwen05-v1 1.58 s, stock 0.5B 1.90 s per answer (target
   ≤ 2.5 s); engine `ai_explain` tests green.
   **AI1.1g-1 done (Q8_0 on AVX2):** `Kernel::dot_v3` override + `dot_q8_0_v3` (sign trick,
   `vpmaddubsw` / `vpmaddwd`, f32 lanes across blocks) via pulp's safe V3 intrinsics + bytemuck
   casts, chosen at run time (`V3::try_new`, off under `FILMCRAFT_LLM_SIMD=baseline`). Synthetic
   960 × 2560: 1 thread **91.5 µs** (portable 205, candle flagged 110), 12 threads **26 µs**
   (candle flagged 41.7). 958-token prefill on the 360M tune 8.3 → **5.2 s**. Exactness vs candle
   now only at Baseline (tests adjusted). **AI1.1g-2 done:** attention `dot` in 8 lanes and a
   4-token register-blocked `dot4_v3` (bit-identical per lane): 958-token prefill 5.2 → **4.2 s**
   (profile before: MLP + wo 2.9 s, qkv 0.8 s, attention 1.0 s; candle no-flag 54.7 s). Remaining:
   **AI1.1g-3 done (Q4_K / Q6_K on AVX2):** Q4_K straight `vpmaddubsw` on the unsigned nibbles;
   Q6_K on the unsigned 0..63 values with the −32 offset through `bsums` (the sign trick would
   overflow: Q8_K activations reach −128). Synthetic 1024 × 2560, 12 threads: Q4_K **24.3 µs**,
   Q6_K **31.7 µs** (candle flagged 31 / 37). Qwen2.5-1.5B 958-token prefill **50 → 11.9 s**
   (candle flagged 17 s), decode 69 → 45 ms / token; SmolLM2-1.7B top token 10 / 10 steps vs
   candle (real-model bound now 20 %). Tests: portable loop bit-identical to candle's scalar path,
   AVX2 within 1e-5; the global SIMD override is no longer touched by tests (it raced with
   `FILMCRAFT_LLM_SIMD=baseline` runs).
   The AI1.1g regression is closed. Optional later: `dot4_v3` for K-quants, AVX-512 / NEON paths
   (the portable loop covers them today).
   **Held-out evaluation on our pass done** (`runs/ai1-own-pass/`, no flag, synthetic): qwen05-v1
   74/75 (identical), smol360-v1 70/75 (recorded 72; set 2 flips 36 / 37 / 38 with summation
   order on threshold cases) at **1.62 s mean per answer** (was ~3 s): OWNER-QUESTIONS **Q22**,
   provisional (a) accept within ±2. This CPU also has AVX-512 (a later V4 path is possible).
   **AI1.2 done (2026-10-13):** the `x86-64-v3` block is gone (Q8 resolved). No Whisper / Kokoro
   weights are on this PC, so their candle f32 ops were timed synthetically
   (`crates/speech/examples/candle_ops_bench.rs`, with vs without the flag): gemm-backed ops equal;
   candle's `conv_transpose1d` (6.7 → 45 ms) and last-dim mean (0.2 → 2 ms) regress without it, so
   Kokoro's `ConvT` (groups 1) and `norm_last` now use gemm (`layers::conv_transpose1d_gemm`,
   `mean_last`: 8.0 / 0.09 ms, tested against candle). Owed: time one real transcription / TTS line
   when weights are on disk (no download without the owner's yes).
   **AI1.3a done (crates/llm):** `Model::generate_streaming(system, user, opts, &mut |so_far| bool)`
   (whole answer so far, half-UTF-8 held back; `false` → `StopReason::Cancelled`; panicking
   callback → `Panicked`), and **prompt-prefix KV reuse** (`Forward::truncate`, `Model.cached`; any
   error / panic forgets the cache; answers identical, tested). Synthetic, real models, only the
   system prompt cached: **first words 0.29–0.31 s** on the 0.5B / 360M models (was ~0.6 s; 1.5B
   0.9 s, 1.7B 1.15 s).
   **AI1.3b done (engine + UI):** `ExplainBackend::generate_streaming` (default: no streaming);
   the job publishes each longer run of **complete sentences that pass the fact check** as
   `Answer::Pending { partial }` (`grade.explainNode` / `ai.explanation` JSON field `partial`);
   unchecked text is never shown, the final answer is still checked whole (a later wrong sentence
   → template). Generation stops when nobody waits (timeout / forgotten). Match tab shows the
   partial. Guide `docs/guide/color/matching.md` updated.
   **AI1.3c done:** background pre-generation: `AiExplain::prefetch` / `can_prefetch`,
   `grade.explainNode {"prefetch": true}`; speculative pending entries, a newer prefetch drops
   older un-asked ones (queued skip, running stop via `wanted()`), an explicit request adopts a
   pending one; the grade panel fires it 0.6 s after the last edit / node change, not while
   playing (`prefetch_explanation`, debounce in egui temp data keyed on revision, clip, node).
   Tested in engine (`a_newer_prefetch_supersedes_and_a_request_adopts`); UI path not
   screenshot-checked (MINIMAL).
   **AI1.4 approved (owner, 2026-10-14, Q24 (a)):** download SmolLM2-135M-Instruct and do AI1.4
   (keep the "fast" tune only if it passes; doesn't block `ai-1`).
   **AI1.5 done:** new L1 crate `crates/ml` (`filmcraft-ml`): `simd::level()`, `job::{guarded,
   Cancel, JobError, panic_message}`, feature `candle` → `ops::{conv_transpose1d, mean_last}`
   (moved from Kokoro; tts's `kokoro` feature enables it). README says how to time a new
   model's candle ops. A2 / G3 build on it.
   **AI1.6 in progress:** `explain_traindata --subtle`, held-out set 3 (`eval_cases_v3`,
   `--set 3`), data-v2 and the training run are described in TRAINING.md "smol360-v2"
   (COACH-QUESTIONS has no entries yet; 6.7b TNR is clip-level, not something the node

   (`session.log`, `train.log`); weights → `models/tuned/smol360-v2/`. If a run died, rerun

   Then acceptance (both held-out sets, ≤ 2.5 s mean,
   first words < 0.5 s ✓ 0.3 s, no crash without / with a corrupt model) → tag `ai-1`.
   **AI1.1 (rest):** own quantized forward pass in `crates/llm` (from candle's MIT / Apache-2.0 code,

1. **(a) 6.2K 4:2:2 graded → 4:2:0 file, target ≥ 24 fps.** Intel decodes (AMD can't decode
   4:2:2) → Y210 / P210 hand-off → AMD renders + encodes. The ~104 MB frame copy must overlap
   decode (off the serial chain). Start by measuring where the time goes today with
   `FILMCRAFT_EXPORT_TRACE=1` / `FILMCRAFT_EXPORT_GPU_PROBE=1` on `xm5_422_2997_20s.mov` (or a
   full-length equivalent in `target/fixtures`); relevant code: `crates/export/src/lanes/`,
   the hand-off in `va_accel::ZeroCopyAccel` (`handoff_scales`, read-back mode).
2. **(b) 6.2K 4:2:2 graded → 4:2:2 HEVC master:** move RGB → 4:2:2 from the CPU to the GPU first
   (today 2.7 fps; `RenderMode::of` in `crates/export/src/job.rs`), then measure honestly. Only
   Intel encodes 4:2:2 (`EncSlice`, no low-power Main422_10), expect ~10–15 fps. Bounded (≈ 1–2 h)
   levers: Intel speed / quality level, two Intel encode sessions on chunks, and our CPU ProRes 422
   HQ encoder at 6.2K for comparison. Short of 24 → OWNER-QUESTIONS FYI with numbers + ProRes.
2b. **Fused graded passes (§1a.71; Q19 (b), Q21):** one full-f32 pass per clip for the colour chain
   + per-pixel effects, Unsharp's passes combined; output within the AMD / Intel parity tolerance
   of today's path. Aims: Intel 6.2K graded ~8–10 fps (from ~3.5), 6.2K graded export ≥ 24 fps
   with both GPUs, lower render time in (a). ≈ 15–25 h. Do it after measuring (a), before (a)'s
   final numbers.

   `docs/fork-benchmarks.md`. GPU code (WGSL RGB → 4:2:2) → GPU parity on AMD and Intel.

`comp-2160`) shows 75 % / 91 % drops: ~400 ms CPU per frame because Media In nodes render their source on the CPU
(`comp::project::ProjectSources::media_in` → CPU image → `Pass::Image`, uploaded every frame). Plan: a new
`Pass::Layers { dst, layers: Vec<PlanLayer> }` that `compile` emits for Media In when the sources can give plan
layers (`CompSources::media_in_layers`, default None → today's image path): `ProjectSources` builds them with the
frame-plan code (`push_item` for the Media In item / the track's items at comp time, scaled to the comp size);
`execute_cpu` runs `plan::execute_layers` into a blank image; the GPU `slot_draw`s the layers (decoded / hardware
frames, zero-copy). Then re-bench. **7b + 7c done 2026-10-14** (`crates/gpu/src/comp.rs` `run_comp`, `comp_tests.rs`, AMD + Intel parity; owed: an Opus
review of `comp.rs` in LEAN or better; `backdrop` / `post` reallocate when comp and frame sizes alternate: pool per
size if the step-13 bench shows it). The design as built (kept for the review): in `crates/gpu` (new `comp.rs`), `GpuCompositor::run_comp(&Program) -> Arc<VideoFrame>`:
slots = `Option<(Texture, View, (w, h))>` per `Tex` + scratch, created like `Self::target` with ACCUM_FORMAT and
`COPY_SRC | COPY_DST`; each slot has a stable placeholder frame (`RgbaF32(Arc::new(Vec::new()))`, program size) and is
**registered in `self.uploads`** under that frame's key (`Uploaded { views: [slot view, dummy ×3], kind: 1,
code_scale 1, texel_scale 1, alpha_scale 0, chroma (w, h), last_used: clock }`) right before every use (the LRU
evicts at 24; never let `upload` see a placeholder unregistered: its empty pixels would be uploaded). Passes:
Image = `render_plan(Layers [PlanLayer::new(image frame, IDENTITY, 1, Normal)], clear)` into the slot (swap
`self.accum` with the slot as transitions do, lib.rs `transition_inputs`); Fill = a 1×1 `rgba_f32` frame scaled by
`Affine::scale(w, h)`; Copy = `copy_texture_to_texture`; Draw / Over = one slot layer with `xform` (Draw: clear
true; Over: clear false, `mode`, `opacity`; hq → `with_quality`); src == dst or Fx = draw into scratch and swap
slot handles; Fx = the slot layer with `fx: Some(LayerFx { size: program size, decimation: 1, ops })`, identity.
Then in `render_plan`, before the accumulator is taken, replace each comp layer's frame with `run_comp`'s out
frame (drop its CPU fallback branch). VRAM: `textures × w × h × 8 B` over `fx::FX_VRAM_BUDGET` → keep the CPU
fallback (log once). Sizes alternate (comp vs frame) so `backdrop` / `post` reallocate: pool them per size if it
shows in the bench. **7c:** parity tests in `crates/gpu/src/tests.rs` per pass kind + comps A / B vs `execute_cpu`
(≤ 1e-3 rel) on AMD and Intel (`MESA_VK_DEVICE_SELECT`, see memory `machine-gpu-render-nodes`).
(Was: **step 7** GPU `PlanStep::Comp` (Draw / Fx / Over on pooled canvases, VRAM check → CPU
fallback, AMD + Intel parity; a Fable/Opus builder per §1a.44 when the mode allows, else do it here);

- **2026-10-17 03:05 EDT (live, on the XPS 16): XPS-SETUP §3 item 10 hand-off failed, fixed (synthetic).** `va_422_handoff` on the laptop: 1 / 5 (the Arc decodes HEVC 4:2:2 10-bit fine, `bframes_422` bit-exact, then every picture read back). Cause (a temporary logger in the test): **NVIDIA's Vulkan driver lists no DMA-BUF modifiers for any 64-bit texel format** (`R16G16B16A16_UNORM` / `_UINT`, `R32G32_UINT`; also none for `R32_UINT`), so importing Intel's linear `Y210` copy as `Rgba16Unorm` failed ("format modifier 0x0 not supported"), `refuse_packed_422` turned the hand-off off for the process and the tests' later streams read back. The Arc takes all of them; NVIDIA takes LINEAR, sampled, for `R16G16_UNORM` / `R16_UNORM` / 8-bit formats. Fix: `interop::import_packed` falls back to the same bytes as `Rg16Unorm` at the even full width ((Y0 Cb) (Y1 Cr) texel pairs); `unpack422` reads two texels per pair when the view is `Rg16Unorm` (uniform `pairs`), refuses any other format. The read-back fallback was already right (log names the import failure) and stays for hostile descriptions. Now `va_422_handoff` 5 / 5 on the RTX 4050 compositor: 18 conformance fixtures bit-exact, 36 pictures drawn in place from Intel's memory (incl. odd 1918×1078); the X-M5 6.2K case skipped until its stand-in exists. Fix: `va::Display` holds an `image_copy` lock across each CPU copy (vaCreateImage → vaGetImage → map → vaDestroyImage in `Surface::read`, and `vaPutImage` in `Surface::write`).
